# Pea protein

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="pea-protein-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="pea-protein-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="pea-protein-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Two retail pea protein powders are the only pea protein products with published single-product metal results, and both contained lead and cadmium.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Arsenic and growing origin have no published data.

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals of concern:</strong>

Lead and cadmium were measured in both pea powders and mercury was quantified in one.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Arsenic was not measured.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

One powder had more than twice the cadmium of the other at nearly equal lead.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Origin, cultivar and isolation process have no published comparison.

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

At its labeled 30 g serving, one powder supplies more lead than the California 0.5 µg/day level.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Test lots for lead, cadmium and inorganic arsenic.

</li>
</ul>
</section>


<a id="decision-summary"></a>
## Pea protein overview

<a id="hmi-ing-004"></a>
<a id="which-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-ingredient-and-its-defined-forms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-005"></a>
<a id="which-sourcing-or-production-decisions-deserve-priority-on-the-available-evidence"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Two retail pea protein powders, bought in Ireland in 2016 and 2017, are the only pea protein products in the Index with published single-product metal results.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Both carried lead, at 22.2 and 22.5 µg/kg, and cadmium, at 40.6 and 15.4 µg/kg; mercury was 4.3 µg/kg in one and below the 0.2 µg/kg quantification limit in the other.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Arsenic was not measured.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Lead matters most for a powder eaten daily: at the first powder's labeled 30 g serving, its lead content works out to 0.666 µg, above California's 0.5 µg/day lead level, while the second powder's labeled 10 g serving works out to 0.225 µg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> These are calculations on two products, not a market estimate. Both cadmium results are below the EU maximum level of 0.10 mg/kg for proteins from pulses.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Commission · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Commission Regulation (EU) 2023/915 of 25 April 2023 on maximum levels for certain contaminants in food and repealing Regulation (EC) No 1881/2006</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Official Journal of the European Union</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

The decisions that matter most for a buyer are which lot to accept and which tests to require, and the evidence limits both.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> No pea protein result reports arsenic or the country where the peas were grown, so lead, cadmium and inorganic arsenic need to be measured on the lot itself.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as pea protein

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers pea protein isolate and pea protein concentrate, the protein fractions made from dried peas and sold as powder or used as an ingredient. The quantified evidence is two powders described by their study as pea protein, measured as sold.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Blends that list pea alongside rice, pumpkin seed or other proteins are kept separate, because a blend result cannot be assigned to one protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Finished protein powders and mixed formulas are covered on the protein powder product page, and rice-derived protein on [[ingredients/rice-protein]].

<a id="heavy-metal-contamination-profile"></a>
## Heavy metal contamination profile of pea protein

<a id="hmi-ing-007"></a>
<a id="which-metals-and-chemical-species-have-been-investigated-in-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-008"></a>
<a id="what-concentrations-detection-frequencies-and-variability-were-observed-in-each-defined-sampling-frame"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-009"></a>
<a id="which-analyte-questions-are-unassessed-access-blocked-conflicting-or-searched-without-eligible-evidence"></a>

The table lists every pea protein result in the Index: 32 rows from two products in one study, covering lead, cadmium and mercury and 13 other elements.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> No row is graded Low, Moderate or High, because two products are too few to form a reference distribution.

Lead was 22.2 and 22.5 µg/kg, cadmium 40.6 and 15.4 µg/kg, and mercury 4.3 µg/kg and below 0.2 µg/kg, all on the powder as sold.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Two products cannot support a median or a typical range.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-pea-protein" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-pea-protein-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of pea protein, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

dataset methods and interpretation</summary><div data-shared-evidence-content><p>Source summaries retain their original statistic, material, basis and censoring. Each source drawer identifies methods and overlapping samples. No matched reference or pooled distribution is inferred.</p></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:pea-protein-9abd3cdd6591:bc5b292e3818"><summary>HMI pea protein Pb reference · Pea protein, powder or ground</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:pea-protein-9abd3cdd6591:bc5b292e3818"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI pea protein Pb reference · Pea protein, powder or ground. Pea protein, powder or ground; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>22.2</td><td>22.4</td><td>22.5</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>22.2</td><td>22.4</td><td>22.5</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>2 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Ring et al. 2021</td><td>2</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for pea protein, powder or ground on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>Ring et al. 2021 is the only compatible sampling family for pea protein, powder or ground on this basis in the completed pea protein ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals" class="internal" data-slug="sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals" data-no-cite-meta>Ring et al. 2021</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-11-02. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:pea-protein-9abd3cdd6591:bc5b292e3818</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-baseline-fallback id="hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:pea-protein-f78a9cb8a2a4:9fdd60c788cf"><summary>HMI pea protein Cd reference · Pea protein, powder or ground</summary><div data-baseline-reference-template="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:pea-protein-f78a9cb8a2a4:9fdd60c788cf"><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Reference and scope</p><p>HMI pea protein Cd reference · Pea protein, powder or ground. Pea protein, powder or ground; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>15.4</td><td>28</td><td>40.6</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>15.4</td><td>28</td><td>40.6</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>2 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Ring et al. 2021</td><td>2</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for pea protein, powder or ground on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach pea protein

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the Index traces how lead or cadmium reach pea protein, whether through the soil, irrigation water, processing equipment or packaging.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A US market screen of 160 protein powders found that plant-based powders averaged three times the lead and five times the cadmium of whey-based powders, but it did not say which plant proteins those powders contained.<sup class="cite">[[sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Clean Label Project · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals">CLP Insights: 2024-25 Protein Powder Category Report (Protein Study 2.0)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Clean Label Project category insights report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> That pattern fits a plant source for both metals, but it is an inference, not a measured pathway for pea protein.


<a id="concentration-ranges-by-country-of-origin"></a>
<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which pea protein sources measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

Within the one study that measured pea protein, the two powders had nearly equal lead, 22.2 and 22.5 µg/kg, but cadmium differed more than twofold, 40.6 against 15.4 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The lowest observed result in that comparison is the second powder for cadmium and mercury; for lead the two are effectively tied.

Compared with the 27 whey powders in the same study, both pea powders were above the whey median lead of 8.6 µg/kg, an Index calculation from the study table, but well below the highest whey value of 88.4 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The first pea powder's cadmium, 40.6 µg/kg, was higher than every one of the 26 whey powders with a quantified cadmium result, whose highest value was 23.2 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> This is a within-study product-type comparison of two pea powders, so it is tentative, not a supported preference for whey over pea.

No study reports pea protein by the country where the peas were grown, so China, Vietnam, the USA and Canada cannot be compared as origins for any metal.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Pea blends produced in the USA, Canada, Belgium and elsewhere in the EU were all below an unprinted detection limit, which cannot rank them, and production country is not growing origin.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Resolving this needs lot results for pea protein linked to the growing region, measured with a stated quantification limit.

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-pea-protein-measured-lowest-in-each-metal" data-anchor="which-pea-protein-measured-lowest-in-each-metal" data-question="Which pea protein measured lowest in each metal?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which pea protein measured lowest in each metal?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

For cadmium and mercury, the second Irish powder measured lowest: 15.4 µg/kg cadmium and mercury below 0.2 µg/kg, against 40.6 and 4.3 µg/kg in the first.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For lead the two powders were effectively the same, 22.5 and 22.2 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Two products from one market do not show that either source is reliably lower.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="can-country-of-origin-pick-a-lower-metal-pea-protein" data-anchor="can-country-of-origin-pick-a-lower-metal-pea-protein" data-question="Can country of origin pick a lower-metal pea protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Can country of origin pick a lower-metal pea protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Not yet: no published pea protein result names the growing region, and the blends with a stated production country were below an unprinted detection limit.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Origin-linked lot results are the evidence that would answer it.

</div>
</details>


<a id="processing-effects"></a>
<a id="derivatives-and-concentrates"></a>
## Processing effects and derivatives

<a id="hmi-ing-016"></a>
<a id="how-do-specified-processing-or-preparation-steps-change-the-measured-metal-endpoints"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-017"></a>
<a id="how-do-derivatives-concentrates-extracts-and-fractions-differ-from-their-parent-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-018"></a>
<a id="do-changes-reflect-removal-redistribution-moisture-or-yield-change-dilution-or-altered-species-or-availability"></a>

No study in the Index compares lead, cadmium, arsenic or mercury in peas before and after protein isolation, or in isolate against concentrate.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Whether isolation removes these metals, leaves them in place or concentrates them as other seed components are removed is therefore unresolved.

Both quantified powders are finished retail products whose processing the study does not describe.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Flavored and blended powders add ingredients that can change the result, so their evidence is kept on the protein powder product page.



<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for pea protein

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

No measured way to lower lead or cadmium in pea protein is in the Index, at the farm or in processing.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The control that can be verified today is testing each lot of finished protein and rejecting lots above the buyer's specification.

Testing detects contamination; it does not remove it. A test is useful only when its quantification limit sits well below the level that matters, which the blend survey without a printed detection limit illustrates.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

A lead-only specification would not have separated the two measured pea powders, because their lead was nearly equal while their cadmium differed more than twofold.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes one limit says little about the others unless each is tested.

No study in the Index reports effects of any control on protein content, yield, taste, stability or waste streams for pea protein.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing pea protein for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

The study that quantified pea protein digested about 0.5 g of powder with nitric and hydrochloric acid and measured it by sector-field ICP-MS, reporting one value per product with stated quantification limits such as 0.2 µg/kg for mercury.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> It did not measure arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A survey that reported every pea blend as below detection did not print its detection limit, so those results cannot show how low the metals were.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> A useful certificate names the accredited laboratory, the method, the quantification limit for each metal and the lot tested, and reports inorganic arsenic separately from total arsenic.



<a id="procurement-and-process-control"></a>
## Buying and verifying pea protein

<a id="hmi-ing-028"></a>
<a id="what-origin-traceability-processing-and-analytical-information-should-a-buyer-request"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-029"></a>
<a id="which-control-or-specification-choices-are-supported-and-which-are-policy-choices-requiring-separate-justification"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-030"></a>
<a id="what-supplier-process-or-test-result-changes-should-trigger-investigation-or-requalification"></a>

A buyer should ask for the name of the testing laboratory and its ISO/IEC 17025 accreditation, the report number for each certificate, the quantification limit for each metal, inorganic arsenic rather than total arsenic alone, and results for the lot being bought. These requests answer the gaps in the published evidence: the arsenic gap, the missing detection limits and the single-product results.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a product sold in California at a 30 g serving, the lead concentration that reaches the 0.5 µg/day level is 16.7 µg/kg (0.5 ÷ 0.030 kg), so a lead quantification limit well below that is needed; at a 10 g serving the figure is 50 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Choosing a specification at or below such a figure is a policy decision for the buyer, not a value set by the evidence.

## Exposure from pea protein

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-033"></a>
<a id="what-conclusions-require-finished-product-measurements-or-a-separate-exposure-assessment"></a>

The study reported the labeled serving of each product: 30 g for the first pea powder and 10 g for the second.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> At those servings the powders supply 0.666 and 0.225 µg of lead and 1.218 and 0.154 µg of cadmium, Index calculations from the study values; California's levels are 0.5 µg/day for lead and 4.1 µg/day for cadmium.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Reproductive and Cancer Hazard Assessment Section · 2001</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001">Proposition 65 Maximum Allowable Daily Level (MADL) for Reproductive Toxicity for Cadmium (Oral Route)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Environmental Protection Agency, Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-cadmium-prop65-madl-2001">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Two or three scoops a day, or pea protein used as one ingredient in a blend, change these figures in proportion to the amount eaten. The study assessed intake at one to three servings a day for a 70 kg adult.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Whether a product needs a California warning depends on its labeled serving, daily use and the law's other provisions, which this page does not assess.
