References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Pea protein FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-pea-protein-contain-lead" data-anchor="does-pea-protein-contain-lead" data-question="Does pea protein contain lead?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does pea protein contain lead?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Both pea protein powders with published results contained lead, at 22.2 and 22.5 µg/kg as sold.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Two products cannot show how common or how high lead is across the market.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-pea-protein-contain-cadmium" data-anchor="does-pea-protein-contain-cadmium" data-question="Does pea protein contain cadmium?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does pea protein contain cadmium?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Yes: the two measured powders had 40.6 and 15.4 µg/kg cadmium.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Both are below the EU maximum level of 0.10 mg/kg (100 µg/kg) for proteins from pulses.<sup class="cite">[[sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Commission · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Commission Regulation (EU) 2023/915 of 25 April 2023 on maximum levels for certain contaminants in food and repealing Regulation (EC) No 1881/2006</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Official Journal of the European Union</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-arsenic-a-concern-in-pea-protein" data-anchor="is-arsenic-a-concern-in-pea-protein" data-question="Is arsenic a concern in pea protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is arsenic a concern in pea protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

It is unknown: no published pea protein result reports total or inorganic arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Pea blends in one survey were below detection for arsenic, but the detection limit was not printed.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Ask for inorganic arsenic on each lot.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-pea-protein-lower-in-metals-than-whey-protein" data-anchor="is-pea-protein-lower-in-metals-than-whey-protein" data-question="Is pea protein lower in metals than whey protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is pea protein lower in metals than whey protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In the one study that measured both, both pea powders were above the whey median for lead, 8.6 µg/kg across 27 whey powders, and one pea powder had more cadmium than any whey powder.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> With two pea products this is a tentative comparison, not a rule.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="is-pea-protein-lower-in-metals-than-rice-protein" data-anchor="is-pea-protein-lower-in-metals-than-rice-protein" data-question="Is pea protein lower in metals than rice protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Is pea protein lower in metals than rice protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

They cannot be compared directly for most metals. Pea protein has lead and cadmium results but no arsenic; rice protein has arsenic results but no quantified lead result.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> See [[ingredients/rice-protein]] for the rice evidence.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-country-grows-the-lowest-metal-peas-for-protein" data-anchor="which-country-grows-the-lowest-metal-peas-for-protein" data-question="Which country grows the lowest-metal peas for protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which country grows the lowest-metal peas for protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No published pea protein result names the growing region, so China, Vietnam, the USA and Canada cannot be compared.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Origin-linked lot results would answer it.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-organic-pea-protein-have-fewer-metals" data-anchor="does-organic-pea-protein-have-fewer-metals" data-question="Does organic pea protein have fewer metals?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does organic pea protein have fewer metals?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No pea-specific data exist; across a US market screen of protein powders of all types, organic products averaged three times the lead and twice the cadmium of non-organic ones.<sup class="cite">[[sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Clean Label Project · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals">CLP Insights: 2024-25 Protein Powder Category Report (Protein Study 2.0)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Clean Label Project category insights report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> That screen did not separate pea protein.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="do-flavored-pea-protein-powders-carry-more-metals" data-anchor="do-flavored-pea-protein-powders-carry-more-metals" data-question="Do flavored pea protein powders carry more metals?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Do flavored pea protein powders carry more metals?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Across all protein powders in a US market screen, chocolate-flavored products averaged four times the lead and 110 times the cadmium of vanilla-flavored products.<sup class="cite">[[sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Clean Label Project · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals">CLP Insights: 2024-25 Protein Powder Category Report (Protein Study 2.0)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Clean Label Project category insights report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/cleanlabel2025-protein-powder-heavy-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> No study separates flavored pea protein.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-do-i-check-a-pea-protein-against-california-prop-65" data-anchor="how-do-i-check-a-pea-protein-against-california-prop-65" data-question="How do I check a pea protein against California Prop 65?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How do I check a pea protein against California Prop 65?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Multiply the lead concentration by the serving weight: 22.2 µg/kg × 0.030 kg = 0.666 µg per 30 g serving, against the 0.5 µg/day level.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment · Not reported</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Lead and Lead Compounds — Proposition 65 Chemical Listing</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">California Office of Environmental Health Hazard Assessment, Proposition 65 Listings</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/oehha-lead-prop65-listing">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Warning requirements depend on more than this arithmetic.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-does-not-detected-mean-on-a-pea-protein-certificate" data-anchor="what-does-not-detected-mean-on-a-pea-protein-certificate" data-question="What does &quot;not detected&quot; mean on a pea protein certificate?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What does &quot;not detected&quot; mean on a pea protein certificate?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

It means the result was below the laboratory's limit, not that the metal is absent. One survey reported pea blends as below detection without printing the limit, so the result shows nothing about how low the metal was.<sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-should-a-pea-protein-suppliers-test-report-include" data-anchor="what-should-a-pea-protein-suppliers-test-report-include" data-question="What should a pea protein supplier&#39;s test report include?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What should a pea protein supplier&#39;s test report include?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The accredited laboratory's name and ISO/IEC 17025 status, the report number, the method, the quantification limit for each metal, inorganic arsenic as well as total arsenic, and the lot tested. The published evidence lacks arsenic and, in one survey, detection limits.<sup class="cite">[[sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Ring G, Sheehan A, Lehane M, Furey A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Development, Validation and Application of an ICP-SFMS Method for the Determination of Metals in Protein Powder Samples, Sourced in Ireland, with Risk Assessment for Irish Consumers</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Molecules 2021, 26, 4347</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ring2021-ireland-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Horváth IL, Kajner G, Galbács G, Csupor D · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Analysis of heavy metal content in protein powders available on the Hungarian market: a reassuring snapshot, but not a reassuring quality guarantee</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Journal of Nutritional Science 14: e49, 1-7</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/horvath2025-hungary-protein-powders-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-the-eu-limit-metals-in-pea-protein" data-anchor="does-the-eu-limit-metals-in-pea-protein" data-question="Does the EU limit metals in pea protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does the EU limit metals in pea protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Yes for cadmium: 0.10 mg/kg for proteins from pulses, and 1.0 mg/kg for food supplements in general.<sup class="cite">[[sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Commission · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Commission Regulation (EU) 2023/915 of 25 April 2023 on maximum levels for certain contaminants in food and repealing Regulation (EC) No 1881/2006</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Official Journal of the European Union</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/eu-2023-915-contaminants-maximum-levels">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The Index has not yet transcribed the EU lead rows that may apply.

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-china-set-a-limit-for-pea-protein" data-anchor="does-china-set-a-limit-for-pea-protein" data-question="Does China set a limit for pea protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does China set a limit for pea protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

GB 2762-2022 has no protein powder row; beans are limited to 0.2 mg/kg lead and 0.2 mg/kg cadmium.<sup class="cite">[[sources/usda-fas2023-china-gb-2762-2022-translation|8]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">USDA Foreign Agricultural Service, FAS China Staff (Beijing) · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/usda-fas2023-china-gb-2762-2022-translation">China Releases the Standard for Maximum Levels of Contaminants in Foods (USDA FAS GAIN Report CH2023-0040, unofficial translation of GB 2762-2022)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">USDA Foreign Agricultural Service, Global Agricultural Information Network (GAIN), Report Number CH2023-0040</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/usda-fas2023-china-gb-2762-2022-translation">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> The 2025 edition replaced it from 2026-09-02, and its bean rows are not yet in the Index.<sup class="cite">[[sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants|9]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">National Health Commission, State Administration for Market Regulation · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants">GB 2762-2025: National Food Safety Standard — Maximum Levels of Contaminants in Foods</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">National Food Safety Standard of the People&#39;s Republic of China</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/chinanhc2025-gb-2762-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="does-fda-closer-to-zero-cover-pea-protein" data-anchor="does-fda-closer-to-zero-cover-pea-protein" data-question="Does FDA Closer to Zero cover pea protein?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Does FDA Closer to Zero cover pea protein?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

No: its action levels cover foods for babies and young children.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda-ctz-Pb-babyfood-2025|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda-ctz-Pb-babyfood-2025">Action Levels for Lead in Processed Food Intended for Babies and Young Children: Guidance for Industry</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, Food and Drug Administration, Human Foods Program</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda-ctz-Pb-babyfood-2025">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
